) in the case
k=6
is complete.

Now we turn to the case
k=5
.
By the definition of
\boldsymbol{u}_{5}^{\mathsf{ao}}
(equation (
117
)),

u_{5,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}=-\mathrm{prox}\Big[\frac{\ell_{\mathsf{a}}^{*}(n\,\cdot\,;1)}{n\zeta_{55}^{u}}\Big]\Big(\frac{\nu_{5,0}+\nu_{5,\mathsf{x}}+h_{5,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}}{\zeta_{55}^{u}}\Big)nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}+\mathrm{prox}\Big[\frac{\ell_{\mathsf{a}}^{*}(n\,\cdot\,;0)}{n\zeta_{55}^{u}}\Big]\Big(\frac{\nu_{5,0}+\nu_{5,\mathsf{x}}+h_{5,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}}{\zeta_{55}^{u}}\Big)(1+nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}).

(133)

Because
\zeta_{55}^{u}\gtrsim n
and proximal operators are 1-Lipschitz
[
BC11
, Proposition 12.27]
,

nu_{5,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}
is
C
-Lipschitz in
h_{5,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}
.
The derivative with respect to
nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}
may diverge linearly in
h_{4,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}
.
Thus, as in the case
k=6
, we must resort to a truncation argument.
We do this by replacing
\nu_{5,0}+\nu_{5,\mathsf{x}}+h_{5,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}
with
\mathsf{Trunc}^{M}(\nu_{5,0}+\nu_{5,\mathsf{x}}+h_{5,i}^{\mathsf{ao}})
in the preceding display,
and following, line for line, the argument used for
k=6
.

Thus, the proof of the lemma is complete.
∎

Lemma

15
has several useful corollaries.

Corollary 1

(Concentration of auxiliary second moments)
.

For
k=5,6
,
under Hypothesis (k-1),

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{G}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}p\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k},\quad

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{n}\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k},

(134)

\displaystyle n\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}n\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k},\quad

\displaystyle\quad\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k},

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{G}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k}^{\top},\quad

\displaystyle\quad\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k}\boldsymbol{Z}_{v,k}^{\top}.

Proof of
Corollary

1
.

The entries of the second-moment functions
\langle\!\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle\!\rangle
and
\langle\!\langle\cdot\rangle\!\rangle
are the empirical averages
order-2 pseudo-Lipschitz functions applied to each coordinate
i
.
Thus, this is a consequence of
Lemma

15
and the
fixed point equations (
SE-fixpt
).
∎

Corollary 2

(Concentration of Cholesky decomposition)
.

For
k=5,6
, under Hypothesis (k-1)

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}\big(n\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\sqrt{n}\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k},\qquad

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}^{\ddagger}\big(n\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\ddagger}/\sqrt{n},

(135)

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}\big(\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{L}_{h,k},

\displaystyle\qquad\mathsf{L}^{\ddagger}\big(\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{L}_{h,k}^{\ddagger}.

We also have

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{\ddagger}\big(n\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}^{\ddagger}/n,\qquad

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{\ddagger}\big(\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k}^{\ddagger}.

(136)

Proof of
Corollary

2
.

We have
n\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{4}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}n\boldsymbol{K}_{g,4}={\boldsymbol{0}}
and
\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{4}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle_{2}=\boldsymbol{K}_{h,2}=\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{\Theta}\rangle\!\rangle
using equation (
79
) and the explicit expression
(\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{u}_{3}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{u}_{4}^{\mathsf{ao}})=({\boldsymbol{0}},{\boldsymbol{0}},-\boldsymbol{1}/n,-\boldsymbol{y}_{1}/n)
and
(\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{v}_{3}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{v}_{4}^{\mathsf{ao}})=(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2},{\boldsymbol{0}},{\boldsymbol{0}})
.
Thus, we have equality of the upper-left
4\times 4
sub-matrices in the lemma.

We check the high-probability approximation for entries
(\ell,\ell^{\prime})

with
\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell
and
\ell\leq k
. We induct on
\ell
. We
use the explicit expressions for the Cholesky decomposition
in
Lemma

3
and the bounds on the fixed point parameters
in
Lemma

8
without explicitly citing them each time.

Assume the result has been shown for the upper-left

(\ell-1)\times(\ell-1)
submatrices. For
\ell^{\prime}<\ell
, the formula

\mathsf{L}(\boldsymbol{K})_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}=\sum_{\ell^{\prime\prime}=1}^{\ell^{\prime}}\mathsf{L}^{\ddagger}(\boldsymbol{K})_{\ell^{\prime}\ell^{\prime\prime}}K_{\ell\ell^{\prime\prime}}
for both
\boldsymbol{K}=n\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle
and
\boldsymbol{K}=\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle
implies
\mathsf{L}(n\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle)_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\sqrt{n}L_{g,\ell\ell^{\prime}}
and
\mathsf{L}(\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle)_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}L_{h,\ell\ell^{\prime}}
because, by the
inductive hypothesis and
Corollary

1
, we have

\mathsf{L}(n\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle)_{\ell^{\prime}\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{\ddagger}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}L_{g,\ell^{\prime}\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{\ddagger}/\sqrt{n}
,
n\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle_{\ell\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{\ddagger}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}nK_{g,\ell\ell^{\prime}}
,

\mathsf{L}^{\ddagger}(\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle)_{\ell^{\prime}\ell^{\prime\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}L_{h,\ell^{\prime}\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{\ddagger}
, and
\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle_{\ell\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{\ddagger}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}K_{h,\ell\ell^{\prime}}
,
and because
L_{g,\ell^{\prime}\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{\ddagger}/\sqrt{n}
,
nK_{g,\ell\ell^{\prime}}
,
L_{h,\ell^{\prime}\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{\ddagger}
, and
K_{h,\ell\ell^{\prime}}

are all
\lesssim 1
. Similarly, the formula
\mathsf{L}(\boldsymbol{K})_{\ell\ell}=\sqrt{K_{\ell\ell}-\sum_{j=1}^{\ell-1}\mathsf{L}(\boldsymbol{K})^{2}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}}

for both
\boldsymbol{K}=n\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle
and
\boldsymbol{K}=\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle
implies
\mathsf{L}(n\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle)_{\ell\ell}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\sqrt{n}L_{g,\ell\ell}
and

\mathsf{L}(\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle)_{\ell\ell}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}L_{h,\ell\ell}
.

Because
\ell>4
,
\ell
is innovative with respect to both
n\boldsymbol{K}_{g}

and
\boldsymbol{K}_{h}
. Moreover,
\sqrt{n}L_{g,\ell\ell}\gtrsim 1
and

L_{h,\ell\ell}\gtrsim 1
, whence, by the high-probability
approximation in the previous paragraph, with exponentially high
probability (depending only on
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}
),
\ell
is innovative with
respect to both
n\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle
and
\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle
. Using the identities

\mathsf{L}^{\ddagger}(\boldsymbol{K})_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}=-\frac{1}{\mathsf{L}(\boldsymbol{K})_{\ell\ell}}\sum_{\ell^{\prime\prime}=\ell^{\prime}}^{\ell-1}\mathsf{L}(\boldsymbol{K})_{\ell\ell^{\prime\prime}}\mathsf{L}^{\ddagger}(\boldsymbol{K})_{\ell^{\prime\prime}\ell^{\prime}}
,
\ell^{\prime}<\ell
and

\mathsf{L}^{\ddagger}(\boldsymbol{K})_{\ell\ell}=-\frac{1}{\mathsf{L}(\boldsymbol{K})_{\ell\ell}}
, which
hold on this high-probability event, the inductive hypothesis and
bounds on the fixed point parameters establish the desired
high-probability approximation for
\mathsf{L}^{\ddagger}(n\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle)
and
\mathsf{L}^{\ddagger}(\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle)
.

The second display in the lemma is a consequence of the first.
∎

Corollary 3

(Bounds on auxiliary objective vectors)
.

For
k=5,6
,
under Hypothesis (k-1),
for
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\leq k
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|

\displaystyle\lessdot C,\qquad

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\|

\displaystyle\lessdot C,\qquad

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{g}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|

\displaystyle\lessdot C,\qquad

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{g}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\|/\sqrt{n}

\displaystyle\lessdot C,

(137)

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{v}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|

\displaystyle\lessdot C,\qquad

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{v}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\|

\displaystyle\lessdot C,\qquad

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{h}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|/\sqrt{n}

\displaystyle\lessdot C,\qquad

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{h}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\|/\sqrt{n}

\displaystyle\lessdot C,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle/\sqrt{n}

\displaystyle\gtrdot c,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle

\displaystyle\gtrdot c.

Proof of
Corollary

3
.

The bounds on
\sqrt{n}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|
,
\|\boldsymbol{v}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|
,
\|\boldsymbol{g}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|
, and
\|\boldsymbol{h}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\|/\sqrt{n}
hold by the first-two lines of
Corollary

1

and the bounds on
\boldsymbol{K}_{g}
and
\boldsymbol{K}_{h}

in
Lemma

8
, and using that
p/n<C
. We then
get the bounds
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\|
,
\|\boldsymbol{g}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\|/\sqrt{n}
,
\|\boldsymbol{v}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\|
,
and
\|\boldsymbol{h}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\|/\sqrt{n}
as consequences, using
their definition in equations (
115
) and
(
118
) and the bounds on the entries of

\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\ddagger\top}/\sqrt{n}
and
\boldsymbol{L}_{h,k}^{\ddagger\top}

in
Lemma

8
. We get the lower bound on
\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle/\sqrt{n}
by observing

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle/\sqrt{n}

\displaystyle\underset{\emph{(i)}}{=}\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{G}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\ddagger\top},\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle_{kk}/\sqrt{n}=[\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\ddagger}\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{G}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle]_{kk}/\sqrt{n}

(138)

\displaystyle\underset{\emph{(ii)}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}}[\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k}^{\top}]_{kk}/\sqrt{n}\underset{\emph{(iii)}}{=}[\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k}^{\top}]_{kk}/\sqrt{n}=L_{g,kk}\zeta_{kk}^{u}/\sqrt{n}\underset{\emph{(iv)}}{\asymp}1,

where equality
(i)
holds by
equation (
115
), approximate equality

(ii)
holds by
Corollary

1
and the bounds on

\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\ddagger}
and
\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k}
in
Lemma

8
,
equality
(iii)
holds
by
Lemma

5
, and the approximation

(iv)
holds by
Lemma

8
. The lower bound
on
\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle
holds similarly.
∎

Corollary 4
.

For
k\geq 3
, under Hypothesis (k-1) we have

\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\qquad\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},

(139)

where, recall,
\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}
and
\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}
are defined
by equations (
118
).

Proof of
Corollary

4
.

By equation (
118
),

\begin{gathered}\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|}=\Big[\sqrt{n}\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\big(\mathsf{L}^{\ddagger}(n\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle)\big)^{\top}\Big]_{\,\cdot\,,k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\Big[\sqrt{n}\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\big(\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\ddagger}/\sqrt{n}\big)^{\top}\Big]_{\,\cdot\,,k}=\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\\
\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|}=\Big[\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\big(\mathsf{L}^{\ddagger}(\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle)\big)^{\top}\Big]_{\,\cdot\,,k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\Big[\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\big(\boldsymbol{L}_{h,k}^{\ddagger}\big)^{\top}\Big]_{\,\cdot\,,k}=\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\end{gathered}

(140)

where in each line the approximate equality
uses
Corollary

2
and the fact that
\sqrt{n}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|\lessdot C
and
\|\boldsymbol{v}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|\lessdot C
for all
\ell
and some
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}
-dependent
C>0

by
Corollary

1
.
∎

H.4
The sequential Gordon inequality

The key tool for showing Hypothesis (k-1) implies Hypothesis
(k) is the sequential Gordon inequality.

We call the objective in equation (
68
) the
primary
objective
, and denote it by
\mathsf{PriObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})
.

Lemma 16

(Sequential Gordon inequality)
.

Assume Hypothesis (k-1). Let
S_{u}=S_{u}(\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\boldsymbol{H}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{1},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2})
be a random,
compact subset of
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
which is, with probability 1, contained
in a ball
\mathsf{B}_{2}({\boldsymbol{0}},C/\sqrt{n})
, and let
S_{v}=S_{v}(\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\boldsymbol{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}})
be a random, compact subset of

{\mathbb{R}}^{p+1}
that is, with probability 1, contained in the ball

\mathsf{B}_{2}({\boldsymbol{0}},C)
, where
C
is any
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}
-dependent constant.
Then

\min_{(v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\in S_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in S_{u}}\;\mathsf{PriObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\gtrdot\min_{(v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\in S_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in S_{u}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v}),

(141)

with the reverse inequality if the minimization and maximization
are reversed. If
S_{u}
and
S_{v}
are almost-surely convex, we
also have

\min_{\boldsymbol{v}\in S_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in S_{u}}\;\mathsf{PriObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\lessdot\min_{\boldsymbol{v}\in S_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in S_{u}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v}),

(142)

with the reverse inequality if the minimization and maximization are
reversed.

Proof of
Lemma

16
.

Let
\boldsymbol{A}^{\|}:=\boldsymbol{A}-\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{A}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}^{\perp}
. The proof of
Lemma

16
is
based on following two facts, to be proved in the sequel:

1.

We have the following equality of distributions:

(\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\;\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\;\boldsymbol{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\;\boldsymbol{H}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\;\boldsymbol{A})\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{d}}}\,(\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\;\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\;\boldsymbol{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\;\boldsymbol{H}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\;\boldsymbol{A}^{\|}+\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}^{\perp}),

(143)

where
{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}}
is independent of everything else.

2.

We have the approximation

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\big\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\|}-(-\boldsymbol{T}_{g}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{T}_{h})\big\|_{{\rm op}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}0.

(144)

First we show how these two facts imply the result. Applying the
standard Gordon inequality (see, for example, Theorem 5.1 in the
paper
[
MM21
]
or Theorem 3 in the
paper
[
TOH15
]
) to the matrix
{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}}

conditionally on
\boldsymbol{A}^{\|},\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\boldsymbol{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\boldsymbol{H}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}
, and then marginalizing over

\boldsymbol{A}^{\|},\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\boldsymbol{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\boldsymbol{H}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\min_{\boldsymbol{v}\in S_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in S_{u}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{v}+\phi(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\leq t\Big)

(145)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{P}\Big(\min_{\boldsymbol{v}\in S_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in S_{u}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}^{\|}\boldsymbol{v}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g},\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\rangle+\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\|\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h},\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\rangle+\phi(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\leq t\Big).

Because
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C
for all
(v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\in S_{v}
and
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}
for all
\boldsymbol{u}\in S_{u}
,

\begin{gathered}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{v}\in S_{v}\\
\boldsymbol{u}\in S_{u}\end{subarray}}\big|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}^{\|}\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}(-\boldsymbol{T}_{g}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{T}_{h})\boldsymbol{v}\big|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\big\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\|}-(-\boldsymbol{T}_{g}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{T}_{h})\big\|_{{\rm op}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}0,\\
\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{v}\in S_{v}\\
\boldsymbol{u}\in S_{u}\end{subarray}}\big|\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g},\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\rangle-\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}\rangle\big|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}0,\\
\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{v}\in S_{v}\\
\boldsymbol{u}\in S_{u}\end{subarray}}\big|\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\|\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h},\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\rangle-\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\|\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}\rangle\big|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}0,\end{gathered}

(146)

where the final two lines use the distributional relations

\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\|^{2}\sim\chi_{\mathsf{rank}(\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}})}^{2}
and
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}\|^{2}\sim\chi_{\mathsf{rank}(\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}})}^{2}
, and
\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}
and

\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}
have rank bounded by
k-1
, where
k
is a finite
constant. The first display in the lemma follows from the previous
two displays. The second display in the lemma follows by an analogous
argument, except that when
S_{v}
and
S_{u}
are convex, the standard
Gordon inequality (see, for example, Theorem 5.1 in the
paper
[
MM21
]
or Theorem 3 in the
paper
[
TOH15
]
) yields the
claim (
145
) with
\leq t
replaced by
\geq t
.

We now turn to proving the claims (
143
)
and (
144
). Beginning with the
claim (
143
), consider any deterministic quantities

\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1},\allowbreak\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1},\allowbreak\{v_{0,\ell}\}_{\ell\leq k-1},,\allowbreak\boldsymbol{M}_{k-1},\allowbreak\boldsymbol{N}_{k-1},\allowbreak\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\allowbreak\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\boldsymbol{e}_{2}
such that

\begin{gathered}\boldsymbol{a}_{\ell}\in\partial_{\boldsymbol{v}}\phi_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell};v_{0,\ell},\boldsymbol{v}_{\ell};\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{e}_{2}),\quad\boldsymbol{b}_{\ell}\in\partial_{\boldsymbol{u}}\Big(-\phi_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell};v_{0,\ell},\boldsymbol{v}_{\ell};\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{e}_{2})\Big),\quad 0\in\partial_{v_{0}}\phi_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell};v_{0,\ell},\boldsymbol{v}_{\ell};\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{e}_{2})\end{gathered}

(147)

for all
\ell\leq k-1
. Then the event that

\mathcal{E}_{1}:=\Bigg\{\begin{gathered}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}=\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1},\quad\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}=\boldsymbol{M}_{k-1},\quad\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}=\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1},\quad\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}=\boldsymbol{N}_{k-1},\\
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{1}=\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\quad\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{1}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\prime},\quad\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}=\boldsymbol{e}_{2},\quad v_{0,\ell}^{\mathsf{po}}=v_{0,\ell}\text{ for $\ell\leq k-1$},\end{gathered}\Bigg\}

(148)

is equivalent to the event that

\mathcal{E}_{2}:=\Big\{\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}=\boldsymbol{M}_{k-1},\quad\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}=\boldsymbol{N}_{k-1},\quad\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{1}=\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\quad\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{1}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\prime},\quad\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}=\boldsymbol{e}_{2}\Big\}.

(149)

Indeed, the inclusion of
\mathcal{E}_{1}
within
\mathcal{E}_{2}
is immediate. The
opposite inclusion of
\mathcal{E}_{2}
within
\mathcal{E}_{w}
follows by the KKT
conditions for the saddle point problem (
68
) and the
identities (
147
). Writing

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{A}^{\|}=\boldsymbol{A}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}+\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}\boldsymbol{A}-\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}\boldsymbol{A}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}},

we see that
\boldsymbol{A}^{\|}
is a function of
\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}
,
\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}
,
\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}
,
\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}
, which we
denote by
f
. Then
\boldsymbol{A}=f\big(\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\allowbreak\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\allowbreak\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\allowbreak\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\big)+\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{A}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}^{\perp}
, so that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Law}(\boldsymbol{A}\bigm|\mathcal{E}_{1})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Law}(\boldsymbol{A}\bigm|\mathcal{E}_{2})=\mathsf{Law}(f(\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1},\boldsymbol{M}_{k-1},\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1},\boldsymbol{N}_{k-1})+\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{A}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}}^{\perp}\bigm|\mathcal{E}_{2})

(150)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Law}(f(\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1},\boldsymbol{M}_{k-1},\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1},\boldsymbol{N}_{k-1})+\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}}^{\perp}),

where the final equality uses that
\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{A}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}}^{\perp}
is independent of
\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}

and
\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}
(for fixed
\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}
,
\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}
), which is
easily checked because
\boldsymbol{A}
has iid standard Gaussian entries.
Note that for all realizations of the matrix
\boldsymbol{A}
and the noise

\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{1},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}
, there is some collection of quantities

\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}\allowbreak\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}\allowbreak\{v_{0,\ell}\}_{\ell\leq k-1},\allowbreak\boldsymbol{M}_{k-1}\allowbreak\boldsymbol{N}_{k-1}\allowbreak\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\allowbreak\boldsymbol{e}_{2}

for which the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
has occurred. Thus, marginalizing the
previous display over the realization of the matrix
\boldsymbol{A}
and
noise
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{1},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}
gives
eq.

143
.

Next, we prove
eq.

144
.
By
eq.

81
, we have
\boldsymbol{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}=\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{v,k-1}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}
and
\boldsymbol{H}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}=\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k-1}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}
. Thus,

\displaystyle-\boldsymbol{T}_{g}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{T}_{h}

\displaystyle=-\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{Z}_{v,k-1}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}+\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}\boldsymbol{A}

(151)

\displaystyle+\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k-1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}+\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}.

We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k-1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k-1}^{\ddagger}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}\underset{\emph{(i)}}{=}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k-1}\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k-1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}

(152)

\displaystyle\underset{\emph{(ii)}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k-1}^{\ddagger}\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\rangle\!\rangle\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}

\displaystyle\underset{\emph{(iii)}}{=}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k-1}^{\ddagger}\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{v,k-1}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}},\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\rangle\!\rangle\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}

\displaystyle\underset{\emph{(iv)}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Big(\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{Z}_{v,k-1}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}-\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}\Big).

Equation
(i)
holds because
\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k-1}\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k-1}^{\top}={\mathbf{I}}_{\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k-1}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k-1}^{\top}=\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k-1}^{\top}
by
Lemma

5
and the
innovation compatibility of
\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k-1}
with
\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k-1}
(the
columns of
\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k-1}^{\top}
lie in the span of the standard basis
vectors corresponding to innovative indices). Approximation

(ii)
holds by
Corollary

1
, the bounds on

\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}
,
\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}
in
Corollary

3
, and the
bounds on
\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k-1}^{\ddagger}
,
\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k-1}^{\ddagger}

in
Lemma

8
. Equation
(iii)
holds by the
definition of
\boldsymbol{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}
. Approximation
(iv)
holds
because, by
Corollary

1
,
\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\rangle\!\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k-1}
, and,
using
Lemma

5
and the innovation
compatibility of
\boldsymbol{Z}_{v,k-1}
with respect to
\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k-1}
,

\boldsymbol{Z}_{v,k-1}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k-1}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k-1}^{\ddagger}=\boldsymbol{Z}_{v,k-1}{\mathbf{I}}_{\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k-1}}=\boldsymbol{Z}_{v,k-1}
. Combining the previous two
displays, we get

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(-\boldsymbol{T}_{g}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{T}_{h})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Big(\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}\boldsymbol{A}+\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}-\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}\Big).

(153)

Now, using
Corollary

2
, we have
\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\mathsf{K}^{\ddagger}(\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\rangle\!\rangle)\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}=\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}
and likewise,
\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k-1}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\mathsf{K}^{\ddagger}(\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}\rangle\!\rangle)\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}\top}=\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}
. Moreover,
\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{{\rm op}}/\sqrt{n}\lessdot C

[
Ver12
, Corollary 5.35]
, whence

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(-\boldsymbol{T}_{g}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{T}_{h})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}\boldsymbol{A}+\boldsymbol{A}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}-\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}}\boldsymbol{A}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{po}}})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol{A}^{\|},

(154)

as desired.
∎

H.5
Properties of the auxiliary objective

Throughout the proofs in this section, we freely invoke the bounds on
the problem parameters or fixed point parameters
from
Lemma

8
and Assumption A1 without citing the
lemma or assumption each time.

Lemma 17
.

For
k=5,6
, under Hypothesis
(k-1)
, we have

\ell_{k}^{*}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{y}_{1},\boldsymbol{y}_{2})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}[\ell_{k}^{*}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{se}};\boldsymbol{w}^{\mathsf{se}},\boldsymbol{y}_{1}^{\mathsf{se}},\boldsymbol{y}_{2}^{\mathsf{se}})].

(155)

Proof of
Lemma

17
.

We handle
k=5
and
k=6
separately. First, we study
k=5
.
Because
\ell_{\mathsf{a}}(\eta;a)
is strongly convex and smooth
(Assumption A1) in
\eta
, so too is
\ell_{\mathsf{a}}^{*}(nu;a)
in

nu
. Moreover,
\ell_{\mathsf{a}}^{*}(0;a)=-\sup_{\eta}\{\ell_{\mathsf{a}}(\eta;a)\}
and

\partial_{\eta}\ell_{\mathsf{a}}^{*}(\eta;a)|_{\eta=0}=\argmin_{\eta}\{\ell_{\mathsf{a}}(\eta;a)
. From Assumption A1, we can conclude that
all of these quantities are bounded above in absolute value by
C
.
This implies that
|\ell_{\mathsf{a}}^{*}(\eta;1)-\ell_{\mathsf{a}}^{*}(\eta;0)|\leq C(1+|\eta|)
. In particular,
\ell_{5}^{*}(\boldsymbol{u}_{6};\boldsymbol{w},-n\boldsymbol{u}_{5},\boldsymbol{y}_{2})
is a

C
order-2 pseudo-Lipschitz function of
n\boldsymbol{u}_{6}
and
n\boldsymbol{u}_{5}
, whence
the result for
k=5
holds by
Lemma

15
.

Next, we study
k=6
. Recalling the expression for

u_{6,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}
in equation (
126
), we may write

\begin{gathered}\ell_{6}^{*}(\boldsymbol{u}_{6}^{\mathsf{ao}};\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{y}_{1},\boldsymbol{y}_{2})=\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{6}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{h}_{2}^{\mathsf{ao}}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}\rangle-nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{po}}\,\frac{(\nu_{6,0}+\nu_{6,\mathsf{x}}-h_{2,i}^{\mathsf{po}}-\varepsilon_{2,i}+h_{6,i}^{\mathsf{po}})^{2}}{\Big(\frac{\zeta_{66}^{u}}{n}\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{po}})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{po}})}}\Big)^{2}},\end{gathered}

(156)

where we use that
-nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}=y_{1,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}
and that

y_{1,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}\in\{0,1\}
. By
Lemma

15
, we have
that
\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{6}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{h}_{2}^{\mathsf{ao}}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{6}^{\mathsf{se}},\boldsymbol{h}_{2}^{\mathsf{se}}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}^{\mathsf{se}}\rangle_{L_{2}}
. Because the
derivatives with respect to
h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}
and
nu_{4_{i}}^{\mathsf{ao}}
grow
quadratically in
h_{2,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}
,
\varepsilon_{6,i}
, and
h_{6,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}
,
its concentration requires a truncation argument. For

(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{ao}},h_{2,i}^{\mathsf{ao}},h_{6,i}^{\mathsf{ao}},nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{ao}},\varepsilon_{i})\mapsto(-nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}\wedge 1)\,\frac{(\nu_{6,0}+\nu_{6,\mathsf{x}}-h_{2,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}-\varepsilon_{2,i}+h_{6,i}^{\mathsf{ao}})^{2}\wedge M^{2}}{\Big(\frac{\zeta_{66}^{u}}{n}\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{ao}})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{ao}})}}\Big)^{2}}

(157)

is
CM^{2}
-Lipschitz, because
c<w(\,\cdot\,)<C
has derivative
bounded by
C
and
|\nu_{6,0}|
,
|\nu_{6,\mathsf{x}}|
,

\zeta_{66}^{u}/n
are bounded above by
C
.
By
Lemma

15
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2nM^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}\wedge 1)\,\frac{(\nu_{6,0}+\nu_{6,\mathsf{x}}-h_{2,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}-\varepsilon_{2,i}+h_{6,i}^{\mathsf{ao}})^{2}\wedge M^{2}}{\Big(\frac{\zeta_{66}^{u}}{n}\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{ao}})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{ao}})}}\Big)^{2}}

(158)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\frac{1}{2M^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[(-nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{se}}\vee 1)\,\frac{(\nu_{6,0}+\nu_{6,\mathsf{x}}-h_{2,i}^{\mathsf{se}}-\varepsilon_{2,i}+h_{6,i}^{\mathsf{se}})^{2}\wedge M^{2}}{\Big(\frac{\zeta_{66}^{u}}{n}\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{se}})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{se}})}}\Big)^{2}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2M^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[-nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{se}}\,\frac{(\nu_{6,0}+\nu_{6,\mathsf{x}}-h_{2,i}^{\mathsf{se}}-\varepsilon_{2,i}+h_{6,i}^{\mathsf{se}})^{2}}{\Big(\frac{\zeta_{66}^{u}}{n}\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{se}})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{se}})}}\Big)^{2}}\Big]+\epsilon(M),

where
|\epsilon(M)|\leq Ce^{-cM^{2}}
by standard Gaussian tail bounds
and the fact that
w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{se}})
is bounded above and below and

-nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{se}}=\in\{0,1\}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{2nM^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}\wedge 1)\,\frac{(\nu_{6,0}+\nu_{6,\mathsf{x}}-h_{2,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}-\varepsilon_{2,i}+h_{6,i}^{\mathsf{ao}})^{2}\wedge M^{2}}{\Big(\frac{\zeta_{66}^{u}}{n}\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{ao}})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{ao}})}}\Big)^{2}}

(159)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2nM^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{ao}})\,\frac{(\nu_{6,0}+\nu_{6,\mathsf{x}}-h_{2,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}-\varepsilon_{2,i}+h_{6,i}^{\mathsf{ao}})^{2}}{\Big(\frac{\zeta_{66}^{u}}{n}\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{ao}})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{ao}})}}\Big)^{2}}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{2nM^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(C+|h_{2,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}|+|\varepsilon_{2,i}|+|h_{6,i}^{\mathsf{ao}}|-M)_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\frac{C}{2M^{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[(C+|h_{2,i}^{\mathsf{se}}|+|\varepsilon_{2,i}|+|h_{6,i}^{\mathsf{se}}|-M)_{+}^{2}\big]\Big)\leq Ce^{-cM^{2}},

where in the first inequality we have
applied
Lemma

8
, in the approximate equality we have
applied
Lemma

15
, and in the second inequality we
have applied standard Gaussian tail bounds. Combining the previous
two displays, we get

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{2n(M\vee M^{2})}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{po}})\,\frac{(\nu_{6,0}+\nu_{6,\mathsf{x}}-h_{2,i}^{\mathsf{po}}-\varepsilon_{2,i}+h_{6,i}^{\mathsf{po}})^{2}}{\Big(\frac{\zeta_{66}^{u}}{n}\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{po}})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{po}})}}\Big)^{2}}

(160)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2(M\vee M^{2})}\mathbb{E}\Big[(-nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{se}})\,\frac{(\nu_{6,0}+\nu_{6,\mathsf{x}}-h_{2,i}^{\mathsf{se}}-\varepsilon_{2,i}+h_{6,i}^{\mathsf{se}})^{2}}{\Big(\frac{\zeta_{66}^{u}}{n}\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{se}})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{se}})}}\Big)^{2}}\Big]\Big|\lessdot Ce^{-cM^{2}}.

That is, for all
\epsilon>0
, the probability that the left-hand
side exceeds
\epsilon+Ce^{-cM^{2}}
is bounded above by

C^{\prime}e^{-c^{\prime}n\epsilon^{r}}
for some
C,c,C^{\prime},c^{\prime},r>0
depending only on

\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}
. Then, taking
M=\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}
, we get

\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{po}})\,\frac{(\nu_{6,0}+\nu_{6,\mathsf{x}}-h_{2,i}^{\mathsf{po}}-\varepsilon_{2,i}+h_{6,i}^{\mathsf{po}})^{2}}{\Big(\frac{\zeta_{66}^{u}}{n}\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{po}})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{po}})}}\Big)^{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}\Big[(-nu_{4,i}^{\mathsf{se}})\,\frac{(\nu_{6,0}+\nu_{6,\mathsf{x}}-h_{2,i}^{\mathsf{se}}-\varepsilon_{2,i}+h_{6,i}^{\mathsf{se}})^{2}}{\Big(\frac{\zeta_{66}^{u}}{n}\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{se}})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{w(h_{1,i}^{\mathsf{se}})}}\Big)^{2}}\Big].

(161)

Because also
\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{6}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{h}_{2}^{\mathsf{ao}}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{6}^{\mathsf{se}},\boldsymbol{h}_{2}^{\mathsf{se}}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}^{\mathsf{se}}\rangle_{L_{2}}
, the proof of
the lemma in the case
k=6
is complete.
∎

Lemma 18

(Value of
\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}
at
(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})
)
.

For
k=5,6
, under Hypothesis (k-1) we have

\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\ell_{k},

(162)

where

\ell_{k}:=-\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\mathsf{se}},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{se}}\rangle_{L_{2}}+\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{k}^{\mathsf{se}},\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{se}}\rangle_{L_{2}}-\mathbb{E}[\ell_{k}^{*}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{se}};\boldsymbol{w}^{\mathsf{se}},\boldsymbol{y}_{1}^{\mathsf{se}},\boldsymbol{y}_{2}^{\mathsf{se}})]+\mathbb{E}[\Omega_{k}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{se}})].

(163)

Proof of
Lemma

18
.

By the definition of
\phi_{k}
and
\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}

(equations (
69
) and (
111
)),

\displaystyle\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})

\displaystyle=-\langle\boldsymbol{g}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{h}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{1}\rangle(\nu_{k,0}+\langle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\mathsf{x}},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle)

(164)

\displaystyle-\ell_{k}^{*}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{y}_{1},\boldsymbol{y}_{2})+\Omega_{k}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}).

Using the definition of
\boldsymbol{g}(\,\cdot\,)
(see
equation (
112
)), we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{g}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1}\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{po},\perp},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle+\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle

(165)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{po},\perp},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle=\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\ddagger\top}\rangle\!\rangle\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{G}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\ddagger\top},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}(\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k})_{k,1:k}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}^{\ddagger}(\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k}^{\top})_{1:k,k}=({\mathbf{I}}_{\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k}^{\top})_{kk}=(\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k}^{\top})_{kk}=\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\mathsf{se}},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{se}}\rangle_{L_{2}},

where the approximate equality in the second line holds
by
Corollary

4
and the high-probability bounds on
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|
and
\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g},\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle
implied by
Corollaries

1
and
2
; the
equality in the second line holds by equations (
114
)
and (
118
); the approximate equality in the third line
holds by
Corollary

1
, the fixed point
relation (
SE-fixpt
); and the remaining equalities in the
third line use equation (
61
), the definition of

{\mathbf{I}}_{\boldsymbol{K}}
from
Lemma

5
, and the fixed
point equations (
SE-fixpt
). We have used bounds on the
fixed point parameters throughout. By a completely analogous argument
we can show that
\langle\boldsymbol{h}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle_{L_{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{k}^{\mathsf{se}},\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{se}}\rangle_{L_{2}}
. Combining these
facts with
Lemma

17
and the fact
\Omega_{k}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}[\Omega_{k}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{se}})]
by
Lemma

15

gives
Lemma

18
.
∎

Lemma 19

(Approximate stationarity of
\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}
at
(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})
)
.

For
k=5,6
, under Hypothesis (k-1) we have

\begin{gathered}\frac{\textup{d}\,}{\textup{d}v_{0}}\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}0,\qquad\frac{\textup{d}\,}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{v}}\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}{\boldsymbol{0}}.\end{gathered}

(166)

Moreover, there exists a random vector
\boldsymbol{\delta}_{u}
such that

\boldsymbol{\delta}_{u}\in\partial_{\boldsymbol{u}}\Big(-\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\Big)\text{
almost surely, and }\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol{\delta}_{u}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}{\boldsymbol{0}}.

(167)

The proof of
Lemma

19
relies on
an approximation for the derivative of each term in
\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}
. The
next two lemmas give these term-by-term approximations. We first
state these two lemmas, then show that they
imply
Lemma

19
, and then prove the two
lemmas.

Lemma 20

(Derivatives of Gordon terms)
.

For
k=5,6
, under Hypothesis (k-1) we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\textup{d}}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{u}}\langle\boldsymbol{g}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{\ell}\zeta_{k\ell}^{u}\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao}},\;\;

\displaystyle\;\;\frac{\textup{d}}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{v}}\langle\boldsymbol{g}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}},

(168)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\textup{d}}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{u}}\langle\boldsymbol{h}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol{h}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}},\;\;

\displaystyle\;\;\frac{\textup{d}}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{v}}\langle\boldsymbol{h}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\sum_{\ell}\zeta_{k\ell}^{v}\boldsymbol{v}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao}}.

Lemma 21

(Derivatives of penalty terms)
.

For
k\geq 5,6
, under Hypothesis (k-1), we have the relations

\begin{gathered}\frac{\textup{d}\,}{\textup{d}v_{0}}\phi_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}0,\qquad\frac{\textup{d}\,}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{v}}\phi_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\frac{\textup{d}\,}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{v}}\phi_{k,v}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\boldsymbol{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\end{gathered}

(169)

provided that
\phi_{k,v}
is defined using solutions to the fixed
point equations (
SE-fixpt
).

Moreover, there exists a random vector
\boldsymbol{\delta}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
such
that
\boldsymbol{\delta}/\sqrt{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}{\boldsymbol{0}}
and almost surely the
following occurs: for all
\boldsymbol{\delta}\in\partial_{\boldsymbol{u}}\phi_{k,u}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\boldsymbol{H}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}};\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{1},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2})
,
also
\boldsymbol{\delta}+\boldsymbol{\delta}\in\partial_{\boldsymbol{u}}\big(-\phi_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\big)
. (This holds
provided
\phi_{k,v}
is defined using the parameters that solve
the fixed point equations (
SE-fixpt
)).

Remark H.2
.

We have a more complicated derivative guarantee for the derivative
with respect to
\boldsymbol{u}
because, when
y_{1,i}=0
,
\ell_{6}^{*}
is the
convex indicator of
u_{6,i}=0
, which is not differentiable.

Proof of
Lemma

19
.

By
Lemmas

20
and
21
and the
definition of
\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}
(see
eq.

111
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\textup{d}\,}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{v}}\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}-\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\zeta_{k\ell}^{u}\boldsymbol{v}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao}}+\frac{\textup{d}\,}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{v}}\phi_{k,v}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\boldsymbol{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}})={\boldsymbol{0}},

where the final equality holds by the KKT conditions for the
problem (
117
). Because
-\langle\boldsymbol{g}(\boldsymbol{u}),\boldsymbol{v}\rangle
and

\langle\boldsymbol{h}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{u}\rangle
have no dependence on
v_{0}
,

Lemma

21
and the definition of
\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}
imply that

\displaystyle\frac{\textup{d}\,}{\textup{d}v_{0}}\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})=\frac{\textup{d}\,}{\textup{d}v_{0}}\phi_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}0.

By the KKT conditions for the problem (
117
),
we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{h}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\zeta_{k\ell}^{u}\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao}}\in\partial_{\boldsymbol{u}}\phi_{k,u}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\boldsymbol{H}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}};(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{j})_{j}).

Taking
\boldsymbol{\delta}
as in Creflem:deriv-penalty yields

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{h}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\zeta_{k\ell}^{u}\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao}}+\boldsymbol{\delta}\in\partial_{\boldsymbol{u}}\Big(-\phi_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\Big).

Then, by
Lemmas

20
and
21
and the
definition of
\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}
, we have

{\boldsymbol{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Big(\frac{\textup{d}}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{u}}\langle\boldsymbol{g}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle-\frac{\textup{d}}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{u}}\langle\boldsymbol{h}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle+\boldsymbol{h}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\zeta_{k\ell}^{u}\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao}}+\boldsymbol{\delta}\Big)\in\partial_{\boldsymbol{u}}\Big(-\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\Big),

(170)

which completes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

20
.

Because
\sqrt{n}L_{g,kk}\gtrsim 1
and
L_{h,kk}\gtrsim 1
,

Corollary

2
implies that with exponentially high probability

\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\neq{\boldsymbol{0}}
and

\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\neq{\boldsymbol{0}}
. On this
event, both
\boldsymbol{g}(\boldsymbol{u})
and
\boldsymbol{h}(\boldsymbol{v})
, as defined in
equation (
112
), are differentiable at

\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}
and
\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}
. We compute

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\textup{d}}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{u}}\langle\boldsymbol{g}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1}\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|}\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle

(171)

\displaystyle\underset{\emph{(i)}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\underset{\emph{(ii)}}{=}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}^{\ddagger}(\boldsymbol{G}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})^{\top}\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}

\displaystyle\underset{\emph{(iii)}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}^{\ddagger}[\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k}^{\top}]_{\,\cdot\,,k}\underset{\emph{(iv)}}{=}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}[\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}{\mathbf{I}}_{\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k}^{\top}]_{\,\cdot\,,k}\underset{\emph{(v)}}{=}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}[\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k}^{\top}]_{\,\cdot\,,k}.

Approximate equality
(i)
uses that
\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle/\sqrt{n}\lessdot C
by
Cauchy–Schwartz and that
\|\boldsymbol{g}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\|/\sqrt{n}\lessdot C
and
\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|\lessdot C
by
Corollary

3
.
Equality
(ii)
uses equations (
114
) and
(
118
). Approximate equality
(iii)
uses

Corollary

1
and the fact that
\sqrt{n}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|_{{\rm op}}\lessdot C
and
\|\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}^{\ddagger}\|_{{\rm op}}/n\lessdot C

by
Corollary

3
. Equality
(iv)

uses
Lemma

5
. Equality
(v)
uses
the definition of
{\mathbf{I}}_{\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}}^{\top}
and the innovation
compatibility of
\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k}
with
\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}
(the columns of

\boldsymbol{Z}_{u,k}^{\top}
lie in the span of the standard basis vectors
corresponding to innovative indices). Bounds are the fixed point
parameters are used throughout. This gives the first approximate
equality in the lemma.

Again using the expression for
\boldsymbol{g}
and
\boldsymbol{h}
in
equation (
112
), we compute
\frac{\textup{d}}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{v}}\langle\boldsymbol{g}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle+\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}.
We have
\sqrt{n}\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle=\sqrt{n}\big[\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}\top}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\big]_{\ell}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\sqrt{n}\big[\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}\big]_{\ell k}=\sqrt{n}L_{g,k\ell},
where the first equality holds by
equation (
118
); the approximate equality holds
by
Corollary

1
, and the second equality holds
by
Lemma

5
(in particular, that

\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\top}={\mathbf{I}}_{\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\top}=({\mathbf{I}}_{\boldsymbol{K}_{g,k}}^{\perp})^{\top}\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\top}=\boldsymbol{L}_{g,k}^{\top}
). By
Corollary

2
,
\sqrt{n}\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|=\mathsf{L}(n\langle\!\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\!\rangle)_{kk}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\sqrt{n}L_{g,kk}.
Because

\|\boldsymbol{g}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\|/\sqrt{n}\lessdot C
and

\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\|/\sqrt{n}\lessdot C
by
Corollary

3
, these
computations imply that
\frac{\textup{d}}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{v}}\langle\boldsymbol{g}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}L_{g,k\ell}\boldsymbol{g}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}=\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}
,
where the equality
uses equation (
114
). This gives the second equation
in the first line of the lemma.

The second line of the lemma follows similarly. For completeness,
we write out the computations, but do not explain the justification
for each line, as they are analogous to the justifications above.
We have

\displaystyle\frac{\textup{d}}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{v}}\langle\boldsymbol{h}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\boldsymbol{v}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle=\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k}^{\ddagger}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}\top}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k}^{\ddagger}[\boldsymbol{K}_{h,k}\boldsymbol{Z}_{v}^{\top}]_{\,\cdot\,,k}=[\boldsymbol{V}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{v}^{\top}]_{\,\cdot\,,k},

(172)

which gives the second equation in the second line of the lemma.
Further, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\textup{d}}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{u}}\langle\boldsymbol{h}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\boldsymbol{h}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle+\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}.

Because
\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{\ell}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}L_{h,k\ell}

and
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}L_{h,kk}
, we get the first equation in the second line of the lemma.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

21
.

By explicit differentiation of equation (
69
) for
k=3,4
,
\frac{\textup{d}\,}{\textup{d}v_{0}}\phi_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})=\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{1}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{se}},\boldsymbol{1}\rangle_{L_{2}}=0,
where the
approximate equality holds by
Lemma

15
and the final
equality holds by the fixed point equations (
SE-fixpt
).
We conclude the first approximate equality in the first display of the
lemma.

By Assumption A1, the function
\Omega_{k}
is differentiable, whence by
explicit differentiation of equation (
69
) for
k=3,4
,
\frac{\textup{d}}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{v}}\phi_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})=\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{1}\rangle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\mathsf{x}}+\nabla\Omega_{k}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\nabla\Omega_{k}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),
where the approximate
equality uses that
\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{1}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}0
and
\|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\mathsf{x}}\|\lesssim C
. By the fixed point equations,
\nu_{k,\mathsf{u}}=0
, whence
\frac{\textup{d}\,}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{v}}\phi_{k,v}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\boldsymbol{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}})=\nabla\Omega_{k}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})
. We conclude the second approximate equality
in the first display of the lemma.

Recall from equations (
3
)
and (
66
) that
y_{1,i}=\mathbb{I}\{\varepsilon_{1,i}^{\prime}=1\}+\mathbb{I}\{\varepsilon_{1,i}^{\prime}=\circ\}\mathbb{I}\{\varepsilon_{1,i}\leq\theta_{1,0}+\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}\}
, and
\boldsymbol{y}_{2}=\theta_{2,0}\boldsymbol{1}+\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}
. Recalling that
\boldsymbol{X}=\boldsymbol{A}+\boldsymbol{1}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\mathsf{x}}^{\top}
,
\boldsymbol{w}=w(\theta_{1,0}\boldsymbol{1}+\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1})

(equation (
8
)), and, by
equation (
104
), that
\boldsymbol{h}_{1}^{\mathsf{ao}}=\boldsymbol{h}_{1}^{\mathsf{po}}=\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}
and
\boldsymbol{h}_{2}^{\mathsf{ao}}=\boldsymbol{h}_{2}^{\mathsf{po}}=\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}
, we see
that

\begin{gathered}\boldsymbol{w}=w((\theta_{1,0}+\langle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\mathsf{x}},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}\rangle)+\boldsymbol{h}_{1}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\\
y_{1,i}=\mathbb{I}\{\varepsilon_{1,i}^{\prime}=1\}+\mathbb{I}\{\varepsilon_{1,i}^{\prime}=\circ\}\mathbb{I}\{\varepsilon_{1,i}\leq\theta_{1,0}+\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}\},\quad\boldsymbol{y}_{2}=(\mu_{2}+\langle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\mathsf{x}},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}\rangle)\boldsymbol{1}+\boldsymbol{h}_{2}^{\mathsf{ao}}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}.\end{gathered}

(173)

By explicit computation using equation (
69
), we see
that
\boldsymbol{\delta}\in\partial_{\boldsymbol{u}}\big(-\phi_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\big)
if
and only if

(\nu_{k,0}+\langle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\mathsf{x}},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle)\boldsymbol{1}-\boldsymbol{\delta}\in\partial_{\boldsymbol{u}}\ell_{k}^{*}\big(n\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{y}_{1},\boldsymbol{y}_{2}\big).

(174)

By comparison of the second-to-last display with
equations (
76
) and (
77
),

\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime}\in\partial_{\boldsymbol{u}}\phi_{k,u}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\boldsymbol{H}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{1},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2})

if and only if

(\nu_{k,0}+\nu_{k,\mathsf{x}})\boldsymbol{1}-\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime}\in\partial_{\boldsymbol{u}}\ell_{k}^{*}\big(n\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{y}_{1},\boldsymbol{y}_{2}\big).

(175)

Because the sub-differential on the right-hand side of the
previous two displays are the same, we see that we can take

\boldsymbol{\delta}=(\langle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\mathsf{x}},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle-\nu_{k,\mathsf{x}})\boldsymbol{1}
.
By
Lemma

15
and because
\langle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\mathsf{x}},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{se}}\rangle_{L_{2}}=\nu_{k,\mathsf{x}}
by the fixed point
equations (
SE-fixpt
), we get that
\langle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\mathsf{x}},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle-\nu_{k,\mathsf{x}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}0
. Because
\|\boldsymbol{1}\|/\sqrt{n}=1
, we conclude that
\boldsymbol{\delta}/\sqrt{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}{\boldsymbol{0}}
, as desired.
∎

Lemma 22

(Curvature of auxiliary objective)
.

For
k=5,6
, under Hypothesis (k-1), with exponentially high
probability
\boldsymbol{u}\mapsto\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};\nu_{0,k},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})
is

cn
-strongly concave and
\boldsymbol{v}\mapsto\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{0,k},\boldsymbol{v})
is
c
-strongly convex.

Proof of
Lemma

22
.

By Assumption A1
\ell_{\mathsf{a}}
is
C
-strongly smooth, whence by
Fenchel-Legendre duality,
\ell_{\mathsf{a}}^{*}
is
1/C
-strongly
convex. By the definition of
\phi_{5}
and
\ell_{5}^{*}
in
equations (
69
) and (
70
),

\phi_{5}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})
is
cn
-strongly concave in
\boldsymbol{u}
.
Moreover,
\ell_{6}^{*}
is
cn
-strongly concave in
\boldsymbol{u}
by
assumption, using that
w_{i}
is bounded below by
c>0
due to
Assumption A1. By the definition of
\boldsymbol{g}(\boldsymbol{u})
and
\boldsymbol{h}(\boldsymbol{v})
in
equation (
112
), we see that

-\langle\boldsymbol{g}(\boldsymbol{u}),\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{h}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{u}\rangle
is concave
in
\boldsymbol{u}
provided
\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\geq 0
. This occurs
with exponentially high-probability by
Corollary

3

(recalling that
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}=\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}
, see
equation (
113
)). Thus with exponentially
high-probability,
\boldsymbol{u}\mapsto\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};\nu_{0,k},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})
is
cn
-strongly concave.

Likewise, by Assumption A1,
\Omega_{k}
is
c
-strong-convex,
whence by the definition of
\phi_{k}
in
equation (
69
),
\phi_{k}
is
c
-strongly convex in

\boldsymbol{v}
. By the definition of
\boldsymbol{g}(\boldsymbol{u})
and
\boldsymbol{h}(\boldsymbol{v})
in
equation (
112
), we see that

-\langle\boldsymbol{g}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}),\boldsymbol{v}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{h}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle
is convex
in
\boldsymbol{v}
provided
\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\geq 0
. This occurs
with exponentially high-probability by
Corollary

3
,
recalling that
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}=\boldsymbol{h}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}
, see
equation (
113
). Thus with exponentially
high-probability,
\boldsymbol{v}\mapsto\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{0,k},\boldsymbol{v})
is
c
-strongly concave.
∎

H.6
Local stability of Gordon’s objective

Let
\phi_{u}:{\mathbb{R}}^{2k+1}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
and
\phi_{v}:({\mathbb{R}}^{p})^{2k}
be any order-2 pseudo-Lipschitz functions. Define
the random sets (which depend on
\boldsymbol{U}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}
,
\boldsymbol{H}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}
,

\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}
,
\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}
, and
\boldsymbol{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao}}
)

\begin{gathered}E_{u}(\epsilon):=\Big\{\boldsymbol{u}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}:\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi\Big((nu_{\ell,i}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp})_{\ell=1}^{k-1},nu_{i},(h_{\ell,i}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp})_{\ell=1}^{k-1},\varepsilon_{2,i}\Big)-\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi\Big((nu_{\ell,i}^{\mathsf{se},\perp})_{\ell=1}^{k-1},nu_{i},(h_{\ell,i}^{\mathsf{se},\perp})_{\ell=1}^{k-1},\varepsilon_{2,i}^{\mathsf{se}}\Big)\Big]\Big|<\epsilon\Big\},\\
E_{v}(\epsilon):=\Big\{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}:\Big|\phi_{v}\Big(\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp},\boldsymbol{v},\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}\Big)-\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi\Big(\boldsymbol{V}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{se},\perp},\boldsymbol{v},\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{se},\perp}\Big)\Big]\Big|<\epsilon\Big\}.\end{gathered}

(176)

Lemma 23

(Local stability)
.

Define
\ell_{k}
as in
Lemma

18
. For
k\geq 5,6
,
under Hypothesis (k-1), we have for sufficiently large constant
C

depending only on
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}
that

\max_{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}}\;\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\ell_{k},

(177)

and

\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\max_{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\min_{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C}\max_{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}\ell_{k}.

(178)

Further, for sufficiently large
C
and appropriately chosen
c,c^{\prime}>0
depending only on
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}
, and any
\epsilon<c^{\prime}

\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}^{c}(\epsilon)\\
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}\end{subarray}}\;\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\lessdot\ell_{k}-c\epsilon^{2},\qquad\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}^{c}(\epsilon)\\
|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\;\max_{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v})\gtrdot\ell_{k}+c\epsilon^{2}.

(179)

Proof of
Lemma

23
.

First, let
\boldsymbol{\delta}_{u}
be as in
Lemma

19
.
Then there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\max_{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}}\;\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\underset{\emph{(i)}}{\lessdot}\max_{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})

(180)

\displaystyle\underset{\emph{(ii)}}{\lessdot}\max_{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}}\left\{\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})+\boldsymbol{\delta}_{u}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})+\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{u}\|\underset{\emph{(iii)}}{\lessdot}\ell_{k}.

Inequality
\emph{(i)}
holds because on the events
|\nu_{0,k}|\leq C
and
\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|<C
, which
Corollary

3

shows have exponentially high probability, the inequality holds
exactly (that is, with “
\lessdot
” replaced by “
\leq
”).
Inequality
(ii)
holds because
\boldsymbol{\delta}_{u}\in\partial_{\boldsymbol{u}}\big(-\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\big)

and by
Lemma

14
, on the event
\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\geq 0
,
\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}
is concave in
\boldsymbol{u}
, and
by
Corollary

3
,
\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\geq 0
with
exponentially high probability (recall
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}=\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}
). Inequality
(iii)
holds
by
Lemma

18
and because
\|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{u}\|/\sqrt{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}0
by
Lemma

19
.

To establish the reverse inequality, let
\boldsymbol{\delta}_{v}=\frac{\textup{d}\,}{\textup{d}\boldsymbol{v}}\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})

and
\delta_{0}=\frac{\textup{d}\,}{\textup{d}v_{0}}\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})
. We follow an
very similar argument as above

\displaystyle\max_{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}}\;\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\underset{\emph{(i)}}{\gtrdot}\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})

(181)

\displaystyle\underset{\emph{(ii)}}{\gtrdot}\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\Big\{\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})+\boldsymbol{\delta}_{v}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})+\delta_{0}(v_{0}-\nu_{k,0})\Big\}

\displaystyle\gtrdot\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})-C\|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{v}\|-C|\delta_{0}|\underset{\emph{(iii)}}{\gtrdot}\ell_{k}.

Inequality
\emph{(i)}
holds because on the event

\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}
, which
Lemma

8

and
Corollary

3
show have exponentially high probability,
the inequality holds exactly (that is, with “
\gtrdot
” replaced
by “
\geq
”). Inequality
(ii)
holds because
by
Lemma

14
, on the event
\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\geq 0
,
\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}
is convex in
(v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})
, and
by
Corollary

3
,
\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\rangle\geq 0
with
exponentially high probability (recall
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}=\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao},\perp}
). Inequality
(iii)
holds
by
Lemma

18
and because
\|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{v}\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}0

and
|\delta_{0}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bullet}}{{=}}0

by
Lemma

19
.

Equations (
178
) follows by a completely
analogous argument, which we present for completeness. There
exists
C
such that

\displaystyle\min_{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C}\;\max_{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v})\geq\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\;\max_{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\geq\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})

(182)

\displaystyle\gtrdot\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\Big\{\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})+\boldsymbol{\delta}_{v}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})+\delta_{0}(v_{0}-\nu_{k,0})\Big\}

\displaystyle\gtrdot\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})-C\|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{v}\|-C|\delta_{0}|\gtrdot\ell_{k},

where the inequalities and approximate inequalities hold by similar
arguments as before. Similarly,

\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\;\max_{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\leq\min_{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C}\;\max_{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v})\leq\max_{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})

(183)

\displaystyle\lessdot\max_{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}}\left\{\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})+\boldsymbol{\delta}_{u}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})\right\}\leq\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})+\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{u}\|\lessdot\ell_{k},

where the inequalities and approximate inequalities hold by similar
arguments as before. Combining the previous two displays gives
equation (
178
).

Now we prove equation (
179
).
By
Lemma

15
, for sufficiently small
c^{\prime}>0
and
any fixed
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
,
\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\in E_{u}(\epsilon/2)
with
probability at least
1-Ce^{-cn\epsilon^{r}}
. When
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}
, which occurs with exponentially high
probability, we have for every
\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}^{c}(\epsilon)
that
\|\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|\geq\epsilon/(2\sqrt{n})
. Thus,

\displaystyle\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}^{c}(\epsilon)\\
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}\end{subarray}}\;\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\leq\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}^{c}(\epsilon)\\
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})

(184)

\displaystyle\underset{\emph{(i)}}{\lessdot}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}^{c}(\epsilon)\\
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}\end{subarray}}\Big\{\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})+\|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{u}\|\,\|\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|-\frac{cn}{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|^{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle\underset{\emph{(ii)}}{\lessdot}\max_{\epsilon/2\leq x\leq C}\Big\{\ell_{k}^{*}+\frac{c\epsilon}{8}\,x-\frac{c}{2}x^{2}\Big\}=\ell_{k}^{*}-\frac{3c\epsilon^{2}}{16}.

Inequality
\emph{(i)}
holds because on the event

\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}
, which
Corollary

3

shows has exponentially high probability, the inequality holds
exactly (that is, with “
\gtrdot
” replaced by “
\geq
”), and
because
\boldsymbol{u}\mapsto\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})
is

cn
-strongly concave with exponentially high-probability
by
Lemma

22
. Inequality
(ii)
holds
because by
Lemma

19
and the argument
preceding the display, with exponentially high probability,
\|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{u}\|/\sqrt{n}\leq c\epsilon/8
and
\|\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|\geq\epsilon/(2\sqrt{n})
for all
\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\in E_{u}(\epsilon/2)
. The first bound in
equation (
179
) follows.

The second bound in equation (
179
) holds
by a similar argument. Indeed, by Hypothesis (k-1), for
sufficiently small
c^{\prime}>0
and any
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
,
\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\in E_{v}(\epsilon/2)
with exponentially high probability. On this
event, for every
\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}^{c}(\epsilon)
we have
\|\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|\geq\epsilon/2
. We thus have

\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}^{c}(\epsilon)\\
|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\;\max_{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\geq\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}^{c}(\epsilon)\\
|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\;\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})

(185)

\displaystyle\underset{\emph{(i)}}{\gtrdot}\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}^{c}(\epsilon)\\
|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\;\Big\{\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}})-\|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{v}\|\,\|\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|-|\delta_{0}|\,|v_{0}-\nu_{0,k}|+\frac{c}{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|^{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle\underset{\emph{(ii)}}{\gtrdot}\max_{\epsilon/2\leq x\leq C}\Big\{\ell_{k}^{*}-\frac{c\epsilon}{8}\,x-\frac{c\epsilon^{2}}{16}+\frac{c}{2}x^{2}\Big\}=\ell_{k}^{*}+\frac{c\epsilon^{2}}{8}.

Inequality
\emph{(i)}
holds because on the event

\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|\leq C
, which
Corollary

3
shows has
exponentially high probability, the inequality holds exactly (that
is, with “
\gtrdot
” replaced by “
\geq
”), and because
\boldsymbol{v}\mapsto\mathsf{AuxObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}};\nu_{k,0},\boldsymbol{v})
is
c
-strongly
convex with exponentially high-probability
by
Lemma

22
. Inequality
(ii)
holds
because by
Lemma

19
and the argument
preceding the display, with exponentially high probability,
\|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{v}\|\leq c\epsilon/8
,
|\delta_{0}|\leq c\epsilon^{2}/(16C)
, and
\|\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\|\geq\epsilon/2

for all
\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{ao}}\in E_{v}(\epsilon/2)
. The first bound in
equation (
179
) follows.
∎

H.7
Proof of
Lemma

13

Recall that
\mathsf{PriObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v}):=\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{v}+\phi_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})
.

H.7.1
Crude bounds on primary optimization saddle point

Lemma 24
.

For
k=5,6
, the primary objective has the following properties.

1.

For any fixed
v_{0},\boldsymbol{v}
, the function
\boldsymbol{u}\mapsto\mathsf{PriObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})
is
cn
-strongly concave in
\boldsymbol{u}
. Thus
also
\boldsymbol{u}\mapsto\min_{(v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\in S}\mathsf{PriObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})
is

cn
-strongly concave in
\boldsymbol{u}
for any set
S
.

2.

For any constant
M
depending on
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}
, with exponentially
high-probability the function
(v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})\mapsto\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}\mathsf{PriObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})
is
c
-strongly convex on

\max\{|v_{0}|,\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\}\leq M
, where
c
depends only on

\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}
and
M
.

3.

With exponentially high probability,
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{po}}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}
,
|v_{k,0}^{\mathsf{po}}|\leq C
,
\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{po}}\|\leq C
. On
this event,

\mathsf{PriObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\mathsf{po}};v_{k,0}^{\mathsf{po}},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\mathsf{po}})=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}v_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}\\
\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}\end{subarray}}\;\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}\mathsf{PriObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v})=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}|v_{0}|\leq C\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\leq C\end{subarray}}\;\max_{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\leq C/\sqrt{n}}\mathsf{PriObj}_{k}(\boldsymbol{u};v_{0},\boldsymbol{v}),

(186)

and in both places the order of minimization and maximization may be
exchanged.

4.

For any
C>0
depending only on
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}
, there exists
C^{\prime}>0
depending only on
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}
and
C
such that with exponentially
high probability

\displaystyle\text{for any $|v_{0}|\leq C$,}
